- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Persona

        - Rationale: Fine-tuning persona for final 18 turns. VictoryCompetitiveness maximized to 10 for science race desperation. Boldness reduced to absolute minimum (1) - no risks, pure defense. WarBias stays at 1 to avoid any offensive wars. GuardedBias increased to 9 reflecting extreme defensive mentality against all threats. MinorCivWarBias reduced to 1 - no city-state conflicts to distract from spaceship race. All other values unchanged for consistency.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Robotics
        - Rationale: Telecommunications completes in 4 turns. Robotics is ESSENTIAL for the spaceship race - unlocks Spaceship Factory which provides massive production bonuses for spaceship parts. China leads with 33% completion (2 parts). Without Spaceship Factory, we cannot catch up. This is the critical infrastructure technology for finishing spaceship components in the final 12 turns after Robotics completes.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Jerusalem relationship from 100 to 40. Iroquois has insurmountable 362 influence lead with only 8 turns to World Congress. Cannot overcome this gap without massive gold investment better spent on Tyre. Maintaining moderate positive to keep quest opportunities open for future.
        - UpdatedTurn: 205
        
        ### Shedet
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -55
        - Rationale: Shedet HOSTILE: America's ally declared war on us. America stole this with Great Diplomat (163 vs our -60). -60/-55 signals lost cause, focus military solution only. Spies and envoys should not waste time here - go to Kabul/Antwerp instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 227
        
        ### Abernethy
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -45
        - Rationale: ABERNETHY LOST: City-state captured by China (now puppet). We had 314 influence which is now irrelevant. Negative relationship acknowledges this is no longer a diplomatic target but reduces resources wasted here to minimum. Focus redirected to Wittenberg/Melbourne/Harappa.
        - UpdatedTurn: 251
        
        ### Prague
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: PRAGUE ABANDONED: America stole this with Great Diplomat (733 influence vs our -60, at war). Completely unwinnable diplomatically. Setting 0/0 to redirect all resources to winnable city-states (La Venta, Antwerp, Kabul, Seoul). Spy Zinnridi should evacuate if possible.
        - UpdatedTurn: 299
        
        ### Wittenberg
        - Public: 0

Current 70/65 relationship provides no diplomatic benefit while active. Resetting to 0/0 redirects AI attention and resources to contestable city-states like Vancouver or defending La Venta against Open Door threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 308
        
        ### Vancouver

Being hostile to China's ally while maintaining alliance with China is diplomatically inconsistent. Spy Zinnridi should redeploy to Harappa or Kabul instead.

With 4 turns to World Congress, directing all available envoys, Great Diplomats, and spy missions here. Must reclaim this delegate to reach 9 total and tie China/Iroquois.
        - UpdatedTurn: 317
        
        ### Tyre
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: TYRE ABANDONED: My Sphere of Influence proposal here will fail since Polynesia (enemy at war) is the ally at 130 vs my -60 (war status). Diplomatic recovery impossible - no resources should be wasted here. Focus on Harappa/Manila instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 332
        
        ### Seoul
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: SEOUL CRITICAL DEFENSE: Maintaining 10th delegate base. Currently strong ally at 988 influence. China has 394 influence - secondary threat. Must defend against Great Diplomat attacks. Losing Seoul drops me to 9 delegates and eliminates diplomatic victory chances. Absolute priority defense alongside Antwerp.
        - UpdatedTurn: 348
        
        ### Manila
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: MANILA FULLY ABANDONED: Siam is ally at 816 vs my 640 influence - insurmountable gap. Spy Fierelus must evacuate immediately and redeploy to Melbourne to rig elections for the trade route quest completion. No resources should be wasted here.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Harappa
        - Public: 0

        - Rationale: HARAPPA DEPRIORITIZED: Mongolia's Great Diplomat created insurmountable 733 vs 455 influence gap (278 point lead). With diplomatic victory no longer achievable, spending resources here is inefficient. Resetting to 0/0 to focus on defensive spy operations against China/America instead of unwinnable city-state contests.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Mongolia reset to 0/0. They stole Harappa with Great Diplomat, have 8 delegates, and are now a diplomatic competitor. However, they also fight Songhai and have limited reach to my territory. Neutral stance avoids antagonizing them unnecessarily while acknowledging rivalry.
        - UpdatedTurn: 373
        
        ### Argos
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: ARGOS ELIMINATED BY OPEN DOOR: Open Door policy enacted on Argos, resetting all influence to 40 and blocking alliances permanently. My 54 influence there provides no delegate benefit. Resetting to 0/0 redirects AI resources away from diplomatic efforts here to maintaining military readiness against America and Iroquois.
        - UpdatedTurn: 429
        
        ### Melbourne
        - Public: 0

        - Rationale: MELBOURNE PERMANENTLY LOST: America's Sphere of Influence proposal succeeded. Melbourne now permanently American ally with 461 influence vs our 367. Diplomatic recovery impossible - no resources should be wasted here. With only 9 delegates vs America's 22, diplomatic victory is impossible. Full pivot to science victory required.
        - UpdatedTurn: 429
        
        ### La Venta
        - Public: 0

        - Public: 0

Since Siam is my Autocracy vassal and ally, diplomatic contest is inappropriate and unwinnable. Resetting to 0/0 to redirect all envoy resources to military production and science infrastructure against imminent American threat.

        ### America
        - Public: -90

        - Rationale: America: Peace treaty EXPIRED 6 turns ago. Existential threat with Information Era tech lead, 23,852 military strength, and Heavy Bomber production. Maximum hostility (-90/-90) ensures AI prepares for active war, maintains nuclear retaliation readiness, and positions all defenses immediately.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### China
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: China: Defensive Pact ally (47 turns remaining). Only protection against American nuclear attack. 100/100 mandatory to maintain alliance despite -999 ideology penalty. Their 16,820 military strength and nuclear deterrent is critical for survival now that American war is active.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Songhai: Frontline ally against America, shares Autocracy ideology. America has been nuking their cities (Sukhothai). Maximum friendship for solidarity and military coordination now that American war has begun.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Siam: Our capitulated vassal providing 3 trade routes with significant science/gold. Their 6 delegates still useful. Maintain loyalty to ensure continued resource extraction during active American war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Polynesia: They have Order ideology (-999), but are distant and not immediate threat. Setting moderate hostility to focus resources on America/Iroquois. They fear our might and are Guarded, suggesting limited offensive capacity against us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -45
        - Rationale: Iroquois peace treaty expires in ~2-3 turns. Increasing hostility from -30/-25 to -50/-45 prepares AI for potential border conflict without provoking premature attack. They have Defensive Pact with America and Order ideology - definite threat once peace expires.
        - UpdatedTurn: 483
        
        ## Strategy

All strategic settings remain optimal: Maximum Science/Production/Spaceship flavors for catching China's 33% lead; absolute Defense/CityDefense/Antiair/Air at 100 against America's 34k military; Offense at 0 to avoid risks. China Defensive Pact (44 turns) remains critical for nuclear deterrence against America. Iroquois peace expires soon but current -50/-45 hostility prepares AI without provoking premature attack. No changes needed.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Seoul:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 484
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: La Venta:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Vancouver:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Manila:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: China:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 462
          - American Sphere of Influence: Shedet:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Kabul:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Focusing on Science Victory**
            
            My current thoughts are entirely consumed by accelerating our spaceship progress. With only 15 turns left, I'm analyzing every avenue to maximize science output and outpace China. Apollo is done, but every available resource must be directed towards our ultimate goal.
            
            **Analyzing Technology Choices**
            
            My focus has shifted to optimizing our technology path. While Telecommunications is nearing completion, I'm confirming Robotics is correctly queued next to unlock the vital Spaceship Factory. I'm also reviewing our policy options and the broader geopolitical landscape, especially America’s advanced tech and military strength, to ensure no critical advantage is overlooked in these final turns.
            
            **Clarifying Diplomatic Pacts**
            
            I'm reassessing our alliances. It seems our defensive pact with China may have expired or never existed, which is a significant oversight. I'm verifying our current diplomatic status with all neighbors to ensure we can solidify key relationships, especially with China, to secure our position.
            
            **Adjusting AI Relationship Values**
            
            I'm recalibrating our diplomatic standing based on observed AI behavior. It appears adjustments to relationship values are necessary to influence their actions, particularly concerning my interactions with America. I've noted our current public and private relationships with all civs and city-states, and I'm reviewing the World Congress proposal for "Global Hegemony." The game's turn limit means I must focus on maximizing victory points if other conditions aren't met by then.
            
            **Accelerating Spaceship Production**
            
            I've realized China is far ahead in spaceship parts, making a direct science victory incredibly challenging with only 15 turns left. However, our cities possess immense production capacity, potentially allowing for rapid assembly of the required parts simultaneously. I'm now focused on re-evaluating the feasibility of this strategy.
            
            **Determining Key Technologies**
            
            I'm now intensely focused on identifying the precise technologies required for all spaceship parts in Vox Populi. My immediate priority is confirming the unlock paths for Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Nuclear Fusion, especially considering our current research queue includes Telecommunications and Robotics. I need to ensure we can rapidly access all necessary components for simultaneous production.
            
            **Researching Crucial Technologies**
            
            My current thoughts are dedicated to mapping the exact technological prerequisites for all spaceship components within Vox Populi. I'm confirming the research paths for Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Nuclear Fusion, given our current queue of Telecommunications and Robotics. The goal is rapid access to all necessary elements for concurrent production.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Conditions**
            
            I am now assessing our progress towards alternative victory conditions, particularly Cultural. My analysis indicates we are "Influential" with Siam, "Popular" with America, and "Familiar" with others, with tourism at 623 per turn. This warrants a deeper examination given the tight timeline for a science victory.
            
            **Evaluating Victory Scenarios**
            
            I'm now concluding that Cultural, Diplomatic, and Domination victories are highly improbable within the remaining turns. My focus must definitively return to a Science Victory, acknowledging the current deficit in spaceship parts and the challenging timeline. I will now thoroughly investigate the feasibility of a rapid, simultaneous production strategy for the required components.
            
            **Preventing Enemy Victory**
            
            My current thoughts are centered on how to actively thwart China's impending Science Victory. With their spaceship parts nearing completion and their overall score so dominant, preventing their win or rushing our own is the only viable path. I need to scrutinize their progress and identify potential weaknesses or opportunities to delay them.
            
            **Assessing Offensive Capabilities**
            
            I am now considering a preemptive strike against China. My analysis shows our military strength exceeds theirs, potentially allowing for disruption of their spaceship progress or even capital conquest, though Beijing's defenses and distance present significant challenges. The primary concern remains a focused Science Victory.
            
            **Confirming Key Technologies**
            
            My current focus is on ensuring the correct technologies are queued for spaceship part production. With Telecommunications nearing completion, I am prioritizing Robotics as the subsequent research to unlock the necessary components. I am double-checking that this aligns with Vox Populi's specific tech tree requirements for these critical parts.
            
            **Revising Victory Focus**
            
            My core thoughts are now locked onto a Science Victory. I've analyzed all other victory paths and found them unfeasible within the remaining turns. My immediate attention is directed towards ensuring the optimal technological research and production strategies are in place to rapidly assemble spaceship components, acknowledging China's lead.
            
            **Investigating Gold Drain**
            
            I'm currently focused on understanding the drastic gold deficit. Despite significant city yields and numerous profitable trade routes, our overall gold per turn is negative. I suspect the "Global Peace Accords" resolution, significantly increasing unit maintenance, is the primary culprit, alongside potentially an excessive number of military units.
            
            **Investigating Gold Drain**
            
            I'm currently examining a severe gold deficit. Despite strong city yields and profitable trade routes, my net gold per turn is significantly negative. I suspect the "Global Peace Accords" resolution, which inflates unit maintenance, combined with a large military, is the primary cause. My immediate concern is the zero gold in the treasury, as this can penalize science output in Civ V.
            
            **Addressing Gold Deficit**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on rectifying our significant gold deficit. I'm considering increasing the Gold flavor priority to 80 to encourage the AI to focus on generating more wealth, and evaluating if city projects like "Wealth" can be run. I also noted our military strength compared to America's, but realized we are not at war and have a non-aggression pact.
            
            **Clarifying Alliance Expirations**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on the imminent expiration of our peace treaty with America in two turns. Given their military strength and declared hostility, this is a critical juncture. I'm reviewing the ongoing global conflicts, particularly China's war with America, to understand potential strategic implications and prepare for any shifts in diplomatic landscape.
            
            **Leveraging Diplomatic Advantages**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on the significant diplomatic advantage we now possess. While China is embroiled in multiple wars, disrupting their science victory push, we remain at peace with all major powers. This unique position is our golden opportunity. I must now focus all efforts on rapid technological advancement and spaceship part production, ensuring we maintain peace for as long as possible to capitalize on this crucial window.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Landscape**
            
            My current focus is on our relationships with other civilizations. America remains highly hostile, making defensive preparedness paramount. The Iroquois are guarded, necessitating caution. China, our friend, is crucial; I'm exploring options to solidify our defensive pact to prevent any betrayal and maintain our offensive capabilities.
            
            **Prioritizing City Production**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing city production for immediate needs. Carthage is correctly prioritizing research, while Utique, Hippo Regius, Gades, and Chicago are completing Nuclear Power Plants. Akwesasme has just finished a Recycling Center. I'm now re-evaluating the production queue for all these cities to ensure they contribute directly to spaceship part assembly or critical science progress.
            
            **Investigating Power Plant Requirements**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on understanding our Nuclear Power Plant construction. It seems counterintuitive we're building them with zero Uranium. I need to confirm if this building is even possible without the resource, or if we're unknowingly importing it. This is critical to understanding our actual production capabilities.
            
            **Clarifying Resource Acquisition**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on clarifying how we're building Nuclear Power Plants without apparent Uranium. I've discovered Siam, our capitulated vassal, recently acquired a Uranium tile. I need to verify if Vox Populi automatically grants us access to vassal strategic resources, or if we must trade for it next turn.
            
            **Deploying Covert Operations**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing our extensive spy network. With eleven agents deployed, I'm reassessing their strategic placement and mission objectives, particularly against America and for rigging elections in key city-states, to gather crucial intelligence and subtly influence global politics.

            **Adjusting Priorities for Victory**
